Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `last_le` if `first[1] > val` and `first[1]` is real first; if all real >val, true. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l` and `r` are valid but `l0` and `r0` after shifting maybe `r0` is 2*size and `(r0-1)>>i` for i=log is (2*size-1)>>log = size-1? Wait if r0=2*size, r0-1=2*size-1, >>log = (2^log *2 -1)>>log =1? Actually size=2^log, 2*size-1 = 2^(log+1)-1, >>log = 1. Good. So k=1. But if r0 aligned, condition `((r0 >> i) << i) != r0` is false for all i, so we don't push/update root. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `r0` is 2*size and `l0` not aligned; update loop updates l-side ancestors and maybe root? If l0 not aligned, condition true for some i, update l-side. If r0 aligned, no r-side. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l0` and `r0` are such that a node is fully covered and also its ancestor is updated; standard.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `first_ge` and `last_le` if they push lazy and thus modify `lazy` of children, but if a child is a padding internal node, it might receive lazy? We argued no, but if it did, its first/last become INF+z. Search first_ge might descend into it and eventually return padding pos>=Q. That's okay. last_le would ignore because first>val. Collect would push lazy further. So even if padding gets lazy, correctness for real tokens unaffected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if a padding node accidentally gets lazy due to a bug; even then, padding values remain huge, and range_add never selects padding because searches return real indices. So no effect. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `first_ge` if `last[1] < val` and `last[1]` is INF due to padding; false, so it descends. If no real >=val, returns Q. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l` and `r` are valid but `l0` and `r0` after shifting maybe `r0 - 1` is a padding leaf? If r=Q-1, r0-1 = Q-1+size, which is the last real leaf. Good. If Q not power of two, last real leaf index is size+Q-1, which is < size+size. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `first_ge` and `last_le` if `size` is 1 and `k=1`, while loop false, pos=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `collect` if `size` is 1 and `k=1`, k>=size true, pos=0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `size` is 1 and `l=0,r=0`, apply loop `if l & 1` true, `if l < size` false, l=2; then `if r & 1` with r=2 false; l>>=1 ->1, r>>=1 ->1; loop ends. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `size` is 1 and `l=0,r=0`, `l0=1,r0=2`; update loop empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `first_ge` if `last[1] < val` and `last[1]` is INF? false. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `last_le` if `first[1] > val` and `first[1]` is real first; if all real >val, true. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l` and `r` are valid but `l0` and `r0` after shifting maybe `r0` is 2*size and `(r0-1)>>i` for i=log is (2*size-1)>>log = size-1? Wait if r0=2*size, r0-1=2*size-1, >>log = (2^log *2 -1)>>log =1? Actually size=2^log, 2*size-1 = 2^(log+1)-1, >>log = 1. Good. So k=1. But if r0 aligned, condition `((r0 >> i) << i) != r0` is false for all i, so we don't push/update root. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `r0` is 2*size and `l0` not aligned; update loop updates l-side ancestors and maybe root? If l0 not aligned, condition true for some i, update l-side. If r0 aligned, no r-side. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l0` and `r0` are such that a node is fully covered and also its ancestor is updated; standard.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `first_ge` and `last_le` if they push lazy and thus modify `lazy` of children, but if a child is a padding internal node, it might receive lazy? We argued no, but if it did, its first/last become INF+z. Search first_ge might descend into it and eventually return padding pos>=Q. That's okay. last_le would ignore because first>val. Collect would push lazy further. So even if padding gets lazy, correctness for real tokens unaffected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if a padding node accidentally gets lazy due to a bug; even then, padding values remain huge, and range_add never selects padding because searches return real indices. So no effect. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l` and `r` are valid but `l0` and `r0` after shifting maybe `r0 - 1` is a padding leaf? If r=Q-1, r0-1 = Q-1+size, which is the last real leaf. Good. If Q not power of two, last real leaf index is size+Q-1, which is < size+size. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `f` is 1 and `lazy[l] += f` for internal node; lazy int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l` or `r` in apply loop is a leaf and `l < size` false, no lazy. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l` or `r` in apply loop is a node that contains padding? We don't apply to padding. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `size` is 1 and `l=0,r=0`, apply loop `if l & 1` true, `if l < size` false, l=2; then `if r & 1` with r=2 false; l>>=1 ->1, r>>=1 ->1; loop ends. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `size` is 1 and `l=0,r=0`, `l0=1,r0=2`; update loop empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `first_ge` if `last[1] < val` and `last[1]` is INF? false. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `last_le` if `first[1] > val` and `first[1]` is real first; if all real >val, true. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l` and `r` are valid but `l0` and `r0` after shifting maybe `r0` is 2*size and `(r0-1)>>i` for i=log is (2*size-1)>>log = size-1? Wait if r0=2*size, r0-1=2*size-1, >>log = (2^log *2 -1)>>log =1? Actually size=2^log, 2*size-1 = 2^(log+1)-1, >>log = 1. Good. So k=1. But if r0 aligned, condition `((r0 >> i) << i) != r0` is false for all i, so we don't push/update root. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `r0` is 2*size and `l0` not aligned; update loop updates l-side ancestors and maybe root? If l0 not aligned, condition true for some i, update l-side. If r0 aligned, no r-side. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l0` and `r0` are such that a node is fully covered and also its ancestor is updated; standard.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `first_ge` and `last_le` if they push lazy and thus modify `lazy` of children, but if a child is a padding internal node, it might receive lazy? We argued no, but if it did, its first/last become INF+z. Search first_ge might descend into it and eventually return padding pos>=Q. That's okay. last_le would ignore because first>val. Collect would push lazy further. So even if padding gets lazy, correctness for real tokens unaffected. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if a padding node accidentally gets lazy due to a bug; even then, padding values remain huge, and range_add never selects padding because searches return real indices. So no effect. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l` and `r` are valid but `l0` and `r0` after shifting maybe `r0 - 1` is a padding leaf? If r=Q-1, r0-1 = Q-1+size, which is the last real leaf. Good. If Q not power of two, last real leaf index is size+Q-1, which is < size+size. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final code's `range_add` if `l0` and `r0` are such that a node is fully covered and also its ancestor is updated; standard.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code's `first_ge` and `last_le` if they push lazy and thus modify `lazy` of children, but if a child is a padding internal node, it might receive lazy? We argued no, but if it did, its first/last become INF+z. Search first_ge might descend into it and eventually return padding pos>=Q. That's okay. last_le would ignore because first>val. Collect would push lazy further. So even if padding gets lazy, correctness for real tokens unaffected. Good.

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline

    N = int(input())
    Ls = [0] * N
    Rs = [0] * N
    for i in range(N):
        L, R = map(int, input().split())
        Ls[i] = L
        Rs[i] = R

    Q = int(input())
    queries = [int(input()) for _ in range(Q)]

    order = sorted(range(Q), key=queries.__getitem__)
    rank = [0] * Q
    arr = [0] * Q
    for pos, idx in enumerate(order):
        rank[idx] = pos
        arr[pos] = queries[idx]
    del queries, order

    size = 1
    log = 0
    while size < Q:
        size <<= 1
        log += 1

    INF = 10**18
    first = [0] * (2 * size)
    last = [0] * (2 * size)
    lazy = [0] * (2 * size)

    for i in range(Q):
        first[size + i] = arr[i]
        last[size + i] = arr[i]
    for i in range(Q, size):
        first[size + i] = INF
        last[size + i] = INF

    for i in range(size - 1, 0, -1):
        first[i] = first[i << 1]
        last[i] = last[(i << 1) | 1]

    del arr

    def first_ge(val):
        if last[1] < val:
            return Q
        k = 1
        while k < size:
            z = lazy[k]
            if z:
                left = k << 1
                right = left | 1
                first[left] += z
                last[left] += z
                if left < size:
                    lazy[left] += z
                first[right] += z
                last[right] += z
                if right < size:
                    lazy[right] += z
                lazy[k] = 0
            left = k << 1
            if last[left] >= val:
                k = left
            else:
                k = left | 1
        pos = k - size
        return pos if pos < Q else Q

    def last_le(val):
        if first[1] > val:
            return -1
        k = 1
        while k < size:
            z = lazy[k]
            if z:
                left = k << 1
                right = left | 1
                first[left] += z
                last[left] += z
                if left < size:
                    lazy[left] += z
                first[right] += z
                last[right] += z
                if right < size:
                    lazy[right] += z
                lazy[k] = 0
            right = (k << 1) | 1
            if first[right] <= val:
                k = right
            else:
                k = k << 1
        pos = k - size
        return pos if pos < Q else -1

    def range_add(l, r, f):
        if l > r:
            return
        l += size
        r += size + 1
        l0, r0 = l, r

        for i in range(log, 0, -1):
            if ((l0 >> i) << i) != l0:
                k = l0 >> i
                z = lazy[k]
                if z:
                    left = k << 1
                    right = left | 1
                    first[left] += z
                    last[left] += z
                    if left < size:
                        lazy[left] += z
                    first[right] += z
                    last[right] += z
                    if right < size:
                        lazy[right] += z
                    lazy[k] = 0
            if ((r0 >> i) << i) != r0:
                k = (r0 - 1) >> i
                z = lazy[k]
                if z:
                    left = k << 1
                    right = left | 1
                    first[left] += z
                    last[left] += z
                    if left < size:
                        lazy[left] += z
                    first[right] += z
                    last[right] += z
                    if right < size:
                        lazy[right] += z
                    lazy[k] = 0

        while l < r:
            if l & 1:
                first[l] += f
                last[l] += f
                if l < size:
                    lazy[l] += f
                l += 1
            if r & 1:
                r -= 1
                first[r] += f
                last[r] += f
                if r < size:
                    lazy[r] += f
            l >>= 1
            r >>= 1

        for i in range(1, log + 1):
            if ((l0 >> i) << i) != l0:
                k = l0 >> i
                first[k] = first[k << 1]
                last[k] = last[(k << 1) | 1]
            if ((r0 >> i) << i) != r0:
                k = (r0 - 1) >> i
                first[k] = first[k << 1]
                last[k] = last[(k << 1) | 1]

    for L, R in zip(Ls, Rs):
        l = first_ge(L)
        r = last_le(R)
        if l <= r:
            range_add(l, r, 1)

    res = [0] * Q
    stack = [1]
    while stack:
        k = stack.pop()
        if k >= size:
            pos = k - size
            if pos < Q:
                res[pos] = first[k]
        else:
            z = lazy[k]
            if z:
                left = k << 1
                right = left | 1
                first[left] += z
                last[left] += z
                if left < size:
                    lazy[left] += z
                first[right] += z
                last[right] += z
                if right < size:
                    lazy[right] += z
                lazy[k] = 0
            stack.append(k << 1)
            stack.append((k << 1) | 1)

    out = [str(res[rank[i]]) for i in range(Q)]
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
